# Manual orphan Slice2 architecture design v2 — PRIVATE, NOT ADOPTED

Design-only review input for Root, 2026-10-21. This replaces the unadopted concrete v1 proposal; the adopted Task2A bootstrap and owned-route Slice1 specs remain binding until Root adopts a supplement. No Source/docs changes, project evaluation, execution, transpilation, test/build, native/HTTP/DB/private-record read, Git mutation or delivery was performed. Only this exclusive0600 artifact is written. Read-only W status is clean `test/manual-orphan-owned-setup-20261021`; Root reports main85d39d8e1a061ed70addf3128bf4ad234681d0dd after PR304. W is the sole Source writer. Both unavailable guards remain unconditional. The recommended architecture is a fixed constructor → isolated runner → direct no-worker integration chain, with separate no-loader provision, activation and mechanical children. Reusing P3 projection or retargeting the fixed production adapter is rejected because it changes the evidence being sought.

`<lexical PG prefix>postgres -D /tmp/setfarm-task6a-pg.<suffix>/data -h 127.0.0.1 -p <port> -k /tmp/setfarm-task6a-pg.<same suffix> -c unix_socket_permissions=0700 -c shared_buffers=32MB -c max_connections=50`

## 3. Exact wire records and limits

All object lists below specify exhaustive insertion order. Encoder rejects proxies before reflection, nonplain/null objects, getters/setters, symbols, missing/extra/nonenumerable keys. All numeric fields are safe integers, never -0. No optional fields. Canonical encoding is UTF8 `JSON.stringify(freshOrderedObject)+'\n'`; decoder accepts Buffer1..16384 bytes including LF, fatal UTF8, strict shape and exact re-encode equality. Reject duplicate keys, BOM/CRLF/noncanonical escapes/order/whitespace/trailing records. Deep-freeze decoded records. Hash exact wire bytes including LF. Arrays occur only in local baselines/inventories, never these frames. Existing probe schema/codec remain unchanged.

H=64 lowercase hex; GitSHA=40 lowercase hex; UUID=lowercase RFC4122v4 with valid variant; OID1..4294967295; process IDs1..2147483647; uid/gid0..2147483647. Decimal stat strings are canonical unsigned decimal<=uint64max, nanoseconds<=signed-int64max. Source files may be zero bytes; executable bytes must be nonzero. All paths in ProcessPin are normalized absolute physical<=4096 UTF8 bytes, no controls; syntax alone is not admission.

| Type | Exact keys/order and constraints |
|---|---|
| ProcessPin | `pid,ppid,uid,pgid,startedAtUTC,executable,executableSha256,commandSha256`; Slice1 timestamp `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.000Z`; last two H |
| DirPin | `dev,ino,uid,gid,mode`; decimal stat strings, uid501,gid20,mode448 for owned private directories |
| FilePin | `dev,ino,uid,gid,mode,nlink,bytes,mtimeNs,ctimeNs,sha256`; nlink1, uid501, mode is permission bits0..4095 with no group/world write; bytes0..16777216; H. Local held comparison additionally retains birthtime and file type |
| Release | `sha,treeHash,buildHash`; GitSHA,GitSHA,H |
| Sources | `maintenance,target,profileSha256,files`; Releases, H, exact file map below |
| Cluster | `root,data,port,postmasterStartedAtUTC,systemIdentifier,serverVersionNum,databaseBaselineSha256,roleBaselineSha256,membershipBaselineSha256`; DirPins, port, exact UTC microsecond timestamp, canonical nonzero uint64 decimal,170000..179999,H,H,H |
| RecordPin | `bytes,sha256`;1..32768,H; runtime-credentials max4096 |
| CreatedI | `installationId,operationId,directory,databaseName,databaseOid,ownerRole,ownerOid,runtimeRole,runtimeOid,sourceSha,schemaCatalogHash,records` |
| ActivatedI | Same CreatedI keys through `schemaCatalogHash`, then `activationCatalogHash,records` |

CreatedI fields: UUID,UUID,DirPin; exact `setfarm_install_<UUID without hyphens>`, OID; exact `sf_owner_<suffix>`,OID; exact `sf_runtime_<suffix>`,OID; fixed T GitSHA,H. Role OIDs distinct; database OID belongs to a different catalog and need not numerically differ from role OIDs. ActivatedI preserves schemaCatalogHash and seven prior RecordPins, adds actual activation catalog hash (activation changes catalog), never silently equates them.

CreatedI.records exact keys: `planned.json,owner-created.json,runtime-created.json,database-created.json,runtime-credentials.json,schema-prepared.json,schema-committed.json`. ActivatedI.records appends `activation-prepared.json,activated.json`. Local actual private reads have total<=256KiB and stock parsers. No credential bytes, origin/migration arrays or caller path are serialized. Independently compare actual full origin,33 stock migration identities, directory identity, names/OIDs/system identifier and both stage catalog hashes.

Sources.files is exactly these11 FilePins, in table order:

| key | Fixed M-relative file |
|---|---|
| constructor | scripts/run-private-postgres-tests.mjs |
| constructorTests | scripts/__tests__/private-postgres-test-cluster.test.js |
| wrapper | scripts/run-execution-attempt-tests.ts |

These11 comparison pins do NOT replace full Git Source/build/package inventories or startup dependency admission. The local retention owners additionally hold the complete tracked Source/build and dist, including Task4 fixture, tsconfig, package metadata and retention scripts. Package inventories and complete resolution-group membership remain authenticated locally, not squeezed into wire hashes.

RunnerSetupFrame exact order:

`schema:'setfarm.manual-orphan-runner-setup.v1', run:H, constructor:ProcessPin, runner:ProcessPin, server:ProcessPin, source:Sources, cluster:Cluster, adminPassword:H`

OwnedPhaseFrame exact order:

`schema:'setfarm.manual-orphan-owned-phase.v1', run:H, constructor:ProcessPin, runner:ProcessPin, parent:ProcessPin, child:ProcessPin, server:ProcessPin, source:Sources, cluster:Cluster, installations:DirPin, purpose, input`

| purpose | Exact input keys | Original issuer → receiver |
|---|---|---|
| provision | `adminPassword` | runner → helper |
| activate | `adminPassword,installation:CreatedI` | runner → helper |
| integration | `installation:ActivatedI` | runner → integration original |

Explicit runner pin removes the ambiguous ancestor gap in v1. For provision/activate/integration parent==runner and runner.ppid==constructor.pid; for mechanics parent is the actual integration, parent.ppid==runner.pid and runner.ppid==constructor.pid. Server.ppid==constructor.pid always. Current independent pins must agree with every carried pin. Retained original issuer handle must agree with child. Nonce correlates this constructor graph and receipt; its value does not authenticate authority. Immediate parent builds a fresh child-bound frame, retains digest and issues once. State `issued→delivered→settled`; no retry/replay/replacement acceptance. A well-shaped changed nonce alone is not a child-side authentication failure; actual parent digest comparison catches mismatch.

Every issuer completes full M/T/bootstrap/dependency acquisition and full recheck BEFORE spawning its recipient. Between spawn and writer.end it only binds the original, performs bounded current process/delivery-pin checks and constructs the fixed frame; no full inventories/imports/resolution run is inserted there. Recipient FD4 reader starts before its own expensive Source acquisition, uses only builtins for stream/strict frame data, and does not follow frame paths. It accepts FIFO/socket only, one frame, real EOF then actual owned-read-handle close,5s from reader initialization. Parent ends its own writer exactly once; actual finish+peer end+close, no inbound data. Schema/purpose crossing refuses. Secret only exists in constructor private0600 password file and setup-frame/local memory. Provision/activate derive their private URL from password plus independently observed port. Integration/mechanical frames omit the secret field entirely. Never log frame/URL/record/raw exception/stack; never export admin/client/handles.

## 4. Bootstrap ordering and finite dependency admission

Implement zero-argument `holdManualOrphanOwnedSourcesV1()` inside the existing inert MJS. It derives fixed roots and returns `{source:Sources,check():void,close():void}` only after actual qualification. No arbitrary root/profile/module/SQL callback. Each process owns its own context, registers each acquired owner before fallible validation, and closes in reverse order once. Acquisition exceptions from legacy retention holders are cleanup-unknown because their API exposes no separate acquisition-cleanup receipt.

Required order:

1. **Before first constructor evaluation**, Root authenticates original NODE/argv/cwd/env/physical M, constructor static closure (`postgres`, builtin-only preflight/journal/helper) and actual package resolution. Constructor entry-time checks cannot undo static imports. Root similarly authenticates the fixed tsx/esbuild orchestration closure before runner/integration launches; immediate parent retains current M Source/package/loader pins through them. This is external original admission, not a field in the frame.
2. Builtin-only helper/receiver derives M/T/account and pins fixed source/metadata/eleven files. Authenticate each `scripts/build-generation-retention.mjs` exact bytes against that release's Git blob and its builtin-only initialization closure BEFORE import. Verify real M current main/origin-main; fixed T commit/tree; fixed profile Git bytes. Use only actual current noarg holder, never selected-deployment/retarget APIs.
3. Import M retention and acquire actual `holdCurrentFinalizedSetfarmSourceBuildV1()`. Immediately retain owner; compare its observation.buildSource. Authenticate stock workspace resolver and normalizer against held build, then call actual resolver and hold its workspace anchor. Acquire T noarg holder similarly after validating fixed T metadata/full output and Git identity. No I5 tail read and no fake production argv. Follow existing production pins' bootstrap data mechanics, not their production-I5 selector.
4. Authenticate each release's full postgres3.4.8/zod4.4.3/yaml2.9.0 inventories independently using the tracked profile's exact lock/archive-provenanced hashes. Preserve16 roots,2048 members/package,8192 entries,depth32,64MiB total,16MiB/file; package count/byte/hash equality, metadata, complete directory membership, no nested package substitution. Hold/recheck nearer package/metadata absences. Preserve build reader caps10000 entries,depth64,32MiB/file,512MiB total and each stock holder4096-FD limit. No cap increases or split-to-evade.
5. Resolve the finite context sets below with known NODE in fixed resolution-only subprocesses, standard import conditions, no target evaluation/hooks, preserving existing64-context cap and1MiB input/output/5s helper limits. Exact metadata, actual parent members, representative same-directory equivalence and all relevant search candidates are required. No missing/extra/duplicate group member. Source/build proves relative compiled bytes; package inventory proves package contents. Resolution checks prove current targets, not loaded-module identity or atomic pathname custody.
6. Only then import/call actual M and T full observers and compare sha/treeHash/buildHash to retained holders. Their static closures exclude db-pg/runtime-config/selection. Hold all contexts across await boundaries. Reobserve actual full observers at named mechanical checkpoints. Source-owner check() checks only named/held Source/build, files, package trees and absences. Each private phase separately composes that with current frame-bound parent/server/root and exact expected environment; it owns the one internal transition from baseline to baseline+derived SETFARM_INSTALLATION_DIR. There is no public selection setter. The phase check must not assert an intentionally obsolete activation-stage snapshot after the owned activation transition.
7. Only after that process's child-bound frame, generated records/root and Source/dependency checks may it internally introduce SETFARM_INSTALLATION_DIR at the phase specified below. It must precede the FIRST selection/db/runtime-config import. No source import through tsx qualifies compiled production code. M/T compiled imports use exact physical file URLs with no query/fragment.

Finite dependency deltas are settled by actual public compiled reads, not left as an implementer choice:

- Existing M observer closure:121 files/79 bare sites/12 groups, from profile. Existing T old-entry union:143 files/82 bare sites/14 groups/29 specified absence paths.
- Actual M62 ordinary-entry union is also143/82, same topology as targetResolution. For M, independently authenticate its own held compiled bytes and full packages, then use that same fixed locator/membership topology (not T byte hashes). Relative to M observer add `dist/db-pg.js→postgres`, `dist/contracts/operational-active-run-status-v1.js→zod`, `dist/execution/v3-seal-capacity.js→zod`. ## 9. Distinct real-child deadlines and joins

Attach original spawn/error/exit/close and every present stdout/stderr/FD4 listener immediately after spawn before fallible hashing/pinning/resume. Missing channel never prevents binding present channels. Original terminal success requires actual exit followed by agreeing close code0/signalnull, natural EOF+close both outputs, and required FD4 finish/end/close with no errors. initdb/server have NO FD4 requirement. Nonzero fully observed exit is a refusal, not automatically a missing-lifecycle unknown. Error, conflicting/duplicate/reordered events, data after EOF, invalid binding, output overflow or deadline burns sticky unknown. First settled promise is immutable history; current snapshot may later become unknown. Recheck it after every await and immediately before/after source-close/final result.

| Purpose | Deadline from spawn unless stated | Per-channel drained-byte cap |
|---|---:|---:|
| Existing probe | unchanged10s | unchanged65536 |
| initdb |60s |262144 |
| server readiness |30s, live-original check | server lifetime262144 |
| server whole lifetime |960s |262144 |
| runner |840s |262144 |
| provision |300s |262144 |
| activation |180s |262144 |
| integration |300s |1048576 |
| each mechanical child |120s |262144 |
| cleanup SQL sequence |30s from start | no raw output |
| explicit SQL end |5s requested API timeout; bounded outer wait | none |
| eligible server SIGINT join |30s from signal | existing lifetime cap |

Hold at most65536 copied diagnostic bytes/channel; incremental hash/count covers all drained bytes even overflow. Receipt markers fit16KiB. No destroy/unref as evidence of close. Deadlines are finite refusal thresholds; no per-run widening. Runner840s contains300+180+300+30 plus overhead; server960s includes readiness and shutdown. Long synchronous Source reads do not evade deadlines: elapsed-time checks at boundaries supplement timers.

On unknown/cancel/limit/journal/client/pin fault: no new phase, no DROP, no server signal; settle/close only own input/client/pin handles as possible, continue bounded drain and report retained current original facts. Do not abandon a pending awaited operation and perform cleanup concurrently. Race timeouts mark unknown; eventual resolution is collected, never green. No SIGKILL/group kill, PID adoption or signal to setup/test children. Natural settlement after deadline supplies diagnostics only. Missing close remains retained unknown. Root owns the actual external constructor graph/capture and must account for unresolved originals. Tests simulate missing forever-close instead of intentionally orphaning a real process.

## 10. Exact cleanup and retention

Only acknowledged provision+activation, actual originals/FDs/source/client closures, cases settled, stable cluster/server/root/Source/OIDs and no uncertainty are cleanup-eligible. A fully closed deterministic assertion mismatch may clean exact resources but still returns CASE_REFUSED; absent closure report or any unknown retains. Partial factory/activation failures never clean. Retain all filesystem diagnostics; no unlink/rm/recursive cleanup.

Runner uses its already captured admin against /postgres. Reverify full settings/system ID/postmaster start/original server; exact DB owner/OID; exact role OIDs/flags/no memberships; captured baselines plus only one generated DB/two roles. Known client backends are only constructor+runner admin PIDs, obtained from their actual connections. Require no generated-DB session and no unknown client backend; standard background types follow existing quiescence list. No pg_terminate_backend/FORCE/sweep/repair.

Order, with identity recheck immediately before every effect, fixed identifier quoting and5s statement/lock timeout:

1. Recheck complete ledger/catalog/session baseline; DROP DATABASE exact generated name outside transaction, without FORCE.
2. Require acknowledged return AND exact name/OID absent. Any lost ack/error stops all subsequent drops even if later read-only diagnostics find absence.
3. Recheck original role identities/memberships; DROP ROLE exact runtime; verify name/OID absent.
4. Recheck owner identity; DROP ROLE exact owner; verify name/OID absent.
5. Require full DB/role/membership baselines equal originals twice100ms apart, same cluster/root/source; await runner admin.end, close retained pins, emit runner receipt and settle original runner.

Constructor independently verifies complete restored baselines plus existing two-sample quiescence requiring only its admin and three DBs. Await its admin.end. Only then call existing bounded `settleOwnedPrivatePostgresV1` on its ORIGINAL server witness, and additionally require the new complete server stdout/stderr witness, normal exit+close and no late fault. Journal.finish/close and root/Source close are final joins; Root still observes original constructor EOF/exit/close. No receipt/PID absence substitutes for those.

If setup was never dispatched, only independently proven no-descendant/unchanged-baseline refusal can use original-server shutdown under the same admitted cleanup scope. Otherwise retain cluster/server/resources on unknown. Drops are ordinary fresh-cluster ownership operations, not atomic OID-based protection against hostile concurrent same-UID writers; exclusive ownership remains mandatory.

## 12. Verdict, deltas and residual risks

**Architecture verdict:** this is a finite implementable eight-file Source design, suitable for Root's full read and explicit adoption. It does not authorize opening/effects today and does not claim runtime readiness. No unresolved schema/API choice is delegated to the implementer. Remaining runtime evidence is precisely original launch/dependencies, fresh built M/canonical/resource/scope conditions and actual fixed-file integration execution; these are admission gates, not permission bits.

Material corrections from v1: activation's necessary internally derived selection before db/runtime-config, intentional stale-stage handling after activation, separate schema/activation catalog hashes, explicit runner ancestor pin, actual relative ps argv, admitted Homebrew opt and tmp lexical/physical identity, settled M82/T85 finite resolution additions, getSql capture only after prepare, no fictitious internal-phase or hidden-client/end-count claim, and explicitly limited nonsecret loader-service evidence. Eleven wire pins remain comparisons beside complete local inventories.

Residual risks are explicit: stock APIs hide internal client/reconciliation history; entry errors collapse phase; held FDs cannot make Node pathname imports atomic; same-UID ownership is not native protection; factory failure can retain a live cluster and partially created objects; tsx/esbuild native service is not covered by direct harness original handles. The pinned nonsecret compiler path is admitted under the existing orchestration dependency/effect/resource boundary; no whole-host or inaccessible-hidden-handle settlement is claimed. Source implementation is independently useful and reviewable. Full-adapter fixed-T drift stays postponed rather than weakening the fixed adapter or old T.


## Binding reviewed clarification (historical preparation label)

# Slice2 architecture v2 — Root clarifications v1, NOT ADOPTED

This narrow supplement corrects sections3 and7 of the private architecture v2, SHA25662245c16529f38a9af1e823c4add5fb25c8363fc370771c409c9a91bbaad461e. Root has not adopted either artifact. V2 remains unchanged. No Source edits, project evaluation, tests/build or runtime effects were performed; only this exclusive0600 clarification is written.

## Post-close behavior: omit the additional entry assertion

Replace section7's requirement “after fulfillment require getSql and entry.assert refusal without reopening” with:

**After the child-owned captured db.pgClose() promise fulfills, require only the actual same namespace db.getSql() to refuse. Do not invoke entry.assert, entry.prepare, database preparation, or any other entry/database assertion after closing.**

The old positive sequence remains: actual first entry.prepare; capture actual public db.getSql() identity; successful actual entry.assert and database.assertSelectedFreshInstallationOperationalDatabaseV1 with identity equality; actual second entry.prepare; repeat both successful assertions and client equality; current Source/selection/record/root/parent/server checks; then one explicit child-owned invocation of that captured database namespace's pgClose. Capture its returned promise, immediately require getSql refusal before awaiting it (synchronous fence), await fulfillment, and require getSql refusal again. Complete current original/FD/source-pin joins before the receipt. Both existing result fields close:'fulfilled' and closedGetter:'refused' keep their exact meaning; receipt shape does not change.

This is the minimal sufficient contract. Task2A's closed-client/no-reopen requirement is evidenced by the actual getter refusal before and after the fulfilled actual close, together with authenticated stock db-pg's CLOSED/epoch fence before its first await. The two actual entry/database assertion pairs already prove the prepared state before closing. Neither adopted Task2A nor Slice1 requires a new post-close entry assertion.

## Exact pin wire types

These definitions clarify section3; object key order remains exactly as v2. No bigint values are serialized. Numeric fields use JSON numbers and must be safe integers, never -0. Decimal-string fields use JSON strings matching 0|[1-9][0-9]* with the bounds below; numbers or alternate encodings in those positions refuse.

| Record | Fields | Exact wire type and bounds |
|---|---|---|
| DirPin | dev,ino | decimal strings,0..18446744073709551615 |
| DirPin | uid,gid | numbers,0..2147483647; actual owned directories require501,20 respectively |
| DirPin | mode | number; owned private directory value448 (0700 permission bits) |
| FilePin | dev,ino | decimal strings,0..18446744073709551615 |
| FilePin | uid,gid | numbers,0..2147483647; Source uid must501; actual observed gid is compared exactly |
| FilePin | mode | number,0..4095; permission bits st_mode & 07777, with group/world write bits absent; exact current Source mode independently checked |
| FilePin | nlink | number, exactly1 |
| FilePin | bytes | number,0..16777216; independently authenticated executable files must be nonempty |
| FilePin | mtimeNs,ctimeNs | decimal strings,0..9223372036854775807 |
| FilePin | sha256 | string, exactly64 lowercase hexadecimal characters |
| RecordPin | bytes | number,1..32768; runtime-credentials.json limit4096 |
| RecordPin | sha256 | string, exactly64 lowercase hexadecimal characters |

DirPin keys: dev,ino,uid,gid,mode. FilePin keys: dev,ino,uid,gid,mode,nlink,bytes,mtimeNs,ctimeNs,sha256. RecordPin keys: bytes,sha256. Local filesystem comparison additionally retains actual type/birthtime and held/named identity as specified in v2; those local fields do not add wire properties.

All remaining v2 contracts and limits stand. Both unavailable guards remain required pending complete-path review and Root's separate actual admission. This supplement settles these two implementation ambiguities without adding an API, frame field or runtime phase.

# Exact adopted canonical baseline DATA contract

### 2. Exact proposed DATA-only canonicalizer

One shared canonicalizer is justified here because constructor initial/restored catalogs and runner independently observed/restored catalogs must produce exactly the same byte digests. Copying sort/key/date rules into both would create an avoidable comparison divergence. This is still a proposed bounded addition to the helper File Map/API, requiring Root adoption after Task3; no current Task3 API/codec/holder modification is implied. Proposed export remains `canonicalizeManualOrphanClusterBaselineV1(value)`, synchronous, `.length===1`, exact call arity1. No optional parameters, overload, SQL, IO, clock, owner, Source check, native observation, or path interpretation.

Input top-level exact own enumerable DATA keys, in canonical output order: `databases`, `roles`, `memberships`. Input property insertion order need not be canonical. Each value must be an ordinary dense Array with Array.prototype and only its own standard length DATA property plus its index DATA properties; indexes must be own enumerable values, no holes, accessors, extra keys or symbols, inherited elements, custom prototypes, subclasses or proxies. Before reflection, reject proxies with existing `types.isProxy`, including the top level, arrays and rows. Check array length descriptors/bounds before enumerating or reading entries. Do not invoke supplied iterators, methods, getters, toJSON or coercions. All rows/top level must have Object.prototype and pass existing `probeOwnDataV1` with the exact keys below. Reject null-prototype objects, classes, Date, Buffer and all accessors. Missing/extra/symbol/nonenumerable row keys refuse. Use descriptor values to build fresh detached rows, never mutate or sort caller arrays.

Exact row contract:

* DB row ordered keys: `name,oid,ownerOid,allowConnections,connectionLimit`. Array length exactly3, names exactly one each of `postgres`, `template0`, `template1`.
* Role row ordered keys: `name,oid,superuser,inherit,createRole,createDb,login,replication,bypassRls,connectionLimit,validUntilUTC`. Array length1..128. This minimum only ensures a nonempty complete baseline; it does not guess stock role count/OIDs.
* Membership row ordered keys: `roleOid,memberOid,grantorOid,adminOption,inheritOption,setOption`. Array length0..256.
* Every OID, including owner/member/grantor, is a JS number accepted by `ownedIntegerV1(value,1,4294967295)`; no strings, bigint, fractional numbers, NaN, infinities or negative zero. DB OID uniqueness is independent of role OID uniqueness; equality across catalogs is allowed. Roles have independently unique name and OID; DB rows likewise independently unique name and OID. Duplicate names with different OIDs and duplicate OIDs with different names both refuse.
* Role names are primitive strings with UTF8 byte length1..63, no U+0000, and `Buffer.from(name,'utf8').toString('utf8')===name` (reject unpaired surrogates). Preserve exact case, whitespace and Unicode bytes; no trimming, normalization, lowercasing or implicit quoting. This is catalog DATA, not identifier interpolation. DB names use the exact three literals above. Numeric-looking names remain strings.
* `allowConnections` and every role/membership flag are primitive booleans; no truthiness coercion. The normalizer preserves each actual flag; it does not assume stock flag defaults. Actual owners separately establish current database/user/server and compare their complete captured baselines.
* DB and role `connectionLimit` are JS safe integer numbers -1..2147483647 inclusive, rejecting negative zero. -1 is preserved. Every other value, including -2, refuses this fresh-cluster baseline contract. No coercion or guessed stock limit.
* `validUntilUTC` is exactly null, `infinity`, `-infinity`, or a primitive27-character timestamp accepted by existing `ownedMicrosecondTimestampV1` with year0001..9999, `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.ffffffZ`. Preserve all six fractional digits. No offset form, JS Date, millisecond-short form, space instead of T, lowercase z, expanded year, or rounded value. SQL producer must request canonical UTC text before postgres type conversion can discard microseconds.
* All DB owner OIDs and all membership role/member/grantor OIDs must occur in the complete input role OID set. Membership roleOid must differ from memberOid. Duplicate membership `(roleOid,memberOid,grantorOid)` triples refuse regardless of flag differences; distinct grantors for the same role/member pair are allowed. Grantor may equal member or role when the actual catalog permits it; the normalizer adds no invented grantor inequality. Do not check a role OID against the DB OID set.

After validation, sort fresh DB/role arrays by Buffer.compare of UTF8 name bytes, then numeric OID; sort memberships by numeric roleOid, memberOid, grantorOid. Rows contain only the primitive values above, in exact ordered keys. Serialize each sorted array with JSON.stringify, no LF. `canonicalBytes` is the sum of UTF8 byte lengths of those three array strings, including their JSON delimiters and escapes, excluding any output envelope. Require <=65536 before hashing/return. These row/name bounds keep all pre-hash construction finite. SHA256 is lowercase64 hex over each entire corresponding UTF8 string. Do not hash an envelope, concatenate arrays for one digest, use locale sorting, or include a trailing LF.

Output has EXACT ordered own enumerable DATA keys:

`databases,roles,memberships,canonicalBytes,databaseBaselineSha256,roleBaselineSha256,membershipBaselineSha256`

The first three are the newly allocated sorted arrays/new ordered-key rows. `canonicalBytes` is a nonnegative safe integer <=65536. The three digests use the exact existing wire field names. Deep-freeze this entire detached plain-object/array tree using existing `freezeOwnedDataV1` only after it has been fully copied and validated; caller objects are never frozen or retained. Return no Buffer, raw JSON strings, functions, owner, closure, status flag or additional schema. A repeated valid call returns a new detached frozen tree; no cache or caller-keyed state.

Check exact arity before inspecting `value`; on wrong arity, invalid value, proxy/accessor attempt or any normalization/hash failure, throw a fresh `Error('MANUAL_ORPHAN_FRAME_REFUSED')` using existing `probeFrameRefusalV1`, without cause/private detail. No partially valid result or fallback digest. Reuse `probeOwnDataV1`, `ownedIntegerV1`, `ownedMicrosecondTimestampV1`, `ownedRequireV1`, `freezeOwnedDataV1` and current crypto machinery; add only the small bounded dense-array/name/duplicate logic required by this DATA contract, without changing those existing helpers' behavior.

Owners must convert actual SQL results to these ordinary detached arrays/rows. They fetch one extra row to detect initial/full-restored overflow:4 DB/129 role/257 membership. This API intentionally accepts only the exact original/restored three-DB baseline. During generated-resource cleanup, the real runner independently validates the complete baseline PLUS exactly the known generated DB/two roles and captured identities before constructing the three-baseline subsets. It cannot silently filter an unknown fourth DB/role/membership to fit this function. The augmented ledger checks remain private real cleanup work, not a new generic canonicalizer mode. No wire schema changes: RunnerSetupFrame.cluster retains precisely databaseBaselineSha256/roleBaselineSha256/membershipBaselineSha256, never canonicalBytes or the retained row arrays.

The earlier43-test figure is a proposal, not final coverage for this clarified API. A subsequent author must add/count finite accessor/proxy/array-hole/duplicate/OID/expiry/refusal/deep-detachment rows required here and report the new exact inventory before Root execution; do not keep43 as a claimed complete count after adopting this refinement.



# Exact adopted setup-only backend DATA contract

## Exact minimal wire refinement

Keep public codec function names and frame schema string unchanged while this capability is globally unavailable, and adopt the following ordered RunnerSetupFrame keys atomically across producer, codec, tests and consumer:

`schema,run,constructor,runner,server,source,cluster,constructorAdminBackend,adminPassword`

The new exact nested ordered keys are `pid,backendStartedAtUTC`:

* pid: JS number, safe integer1..2147483647, no negativezero, accepted by existing ownedIntegerV1(value,1). It is a PostgreSQL backend PID observed on the constructor's own connection, not constructor.pid, runner.pid, server.pid, a connId from postgres callbacks, nor a caller-selected PID.
* backendStartedAtUTC: exact27-byte UTC microsecond string `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ss.ffffffZ`, validated with existing ownedMicrosecondTimestampV1. It comes from that row's backend_start formatted explicitly to UTC text, not a JS Date or local clock. No millisecond truncation, guessed timestamp or refreshed start.
* Require a plain own-DATA object through existing probeOwnDataV1; exact keys/booleans/types/proxy-accessor discipline and canonical UTF8/LF/16384-byte frame limits remain as today. Decoder returns detached deep-frozen DATA through existing machinery. Missing member, extra fields, aliases, wrong PID/timestamp or old payload refuses MANUAL_ORPHAN_FRAME_REFUSED. No optional compatibility path or fabricated default.

The shared Cluster schema, phase schema, integration result and runner result remain unchanged. In particular the three baseline digest names and preexisting row/hash contracts do not change. This confines the wire refinement to the direct constructor-to-runner edge where the observation is needed. The frame's actual canonical bytes/hash now include the new member, and the existing result frameSha256 binding consequently covers it. No authentication is obtained from hashing alone: actual immediate parent/original FD4/Source/cluster validation is still mandatory.

Root must explicitly adopt the compatibility decision before Source edits. The capability is still guarded and has no admitted historical successful payload to preserve, so a coordinated exact-key update under the existing v1 label is smaller than adding a parallel schema/version parser. If Root discovers any admitted persisted/third-party use of this exact schema, do not silently revise it; separately decide a version migration. This preparation found no such use and makes no universal claim about consumers outside the scoped public Source.

## Risk1: PG version admission without extra constructor children

Legacy preconditions calls `execFileSync(PG+tool,['--version'])` for each tool at226–230, runs Git status at223–224 and checks npm at232–233. Do not hide them in initdb observer/journal role, invoke them with spawnSync and call exitCode sufficient, or add an unreviewed purpose.

Place exact PG binary VERSION admission at RootTask7, outside the constructor graph, before any cluster. Root already must authenticate physical opt->Cellar chain, executable hashes/protection and exact version, and own/observe any external version commands it chooses to run. Record those actual results there; this map does not supply a guessed minor version/hash. Initial Node/tsx/postgres package/preload resolution is also RootTask7 admission because constructor statically imports postgres before JavaScript checks. Treat the exact independently admitted Homebrew opt chain as an exception, not every symlink; refuse other aliases/mutations. Never choose a tool from wire/env/PATH, execute an arbitrary caller version command, or substitute the physical spelling for prescribed lexical planner argv.

The actual server raw command/physical executable observation must match the constructor's original child and captured tool facts. This verifies the RUNNING server version/identity; it does not retrospectively prove initdb --version. Initdb's particular version was admitted by Root for the byte-pinned executable before spawn. Keep these claims separate.

No new secret/caller channel is needed to transmit a Root approval boolean. Constructor's first tool pin is an observation, not evidence it can internally rediscover an external human approval. Root is responsible for confirming its admitted exact files/current conditions at external launch under exclusive ownership; constructor pins against subsequent change. If Root wants a programmatically conveyed immutable expected-tool-hash record in addition, it requires an explicit further interface refinement; do not smuggle one through frame/env/private files. Current adopted scope requires external admission plus in-process continuity, and that is the proposed smallest solution.

The complete Source holder/process observer already uses its own bounded read-only resolution/Git/ps mechanisms (for example helper883 starts the existing resolution child; observeProcess1252 uses ps). Do not falsely claim the whole Node process has only three OS descendants total. The fixed constructor OWNED WORKLOAD graph has initdb, server, runner only; existing Source/observation mechanisms retain their distinct already reviewed semantics and limits. This proposal adds no PG version workload child and does not relabel observer subprocesses as owned initdb/server/test.

## Clock, reserved admin and query/close boundaries

NEW MODE uses only process.hrtime.bigint, integer nanoseconds, sticky getter/call/result/backwards fault and elapsed checks. No Date/performance fallback. Private spawn primitive takes its own origin BEFORE native spawn, so initial clock fault =>spawn0; after spawn bind all present lifecycle/stream/FD4 channels before fallible hash/pin/clock; postspawn clock fault retains/drains actual original and stops further phases. Observer-local origin cannot cover native spawn/bind duration. Fixed owner limits are independent of local observer timers. Legacy racePrivateTestDeadlineV1/performance and cancellation.run Promise.race must not drive new-mode cleanup abandonment.

Use new-mode pool max1,idle_timeout0,max_lifetime0, exactly one held reservation. Private onclose ledger is sticky except the explicitly expected final close notification in closing state; connId is never backend PID. New admin operations are serial, no queued/pipelined overlap. At start/end of each query and after each await check cancellation/clock/source/root/originalserver and own backend immutable pair. Actual own identity query returns pg_backend_pid plus its own pg_stat_activity row/backend_start formatted as UTC text, postgres db/current/session user, admitted endpoint, and baseline OIDs. Compare exact pid/start to captured initial pair; no reconnect/re-reserve/refresh.

A query rejection, unexpectedonclose, stale/changed PID/start, cancellation or deadline burns unknown. Keep the in-flight operation promise and collect its eventual result. Do not drop/signal/start a new phase or close a borrowed/new client while the pending operation continues. A bounded wait expiring means retained unresolved work, not permission to claim closure or execute cleanup concurrently. Root accounts for that unresolved client/child graph. All actual SQL settings and own connection identity validations must be complete before receipt/cleanup claims.

Final admin close is a monotonic transition ready->closing->closed OR unknown, not a boolean reset. Require no pending query; stop scheduling permanently, burn reservation ownership before one release call, immediately request end({timeout:5}) on own pool, retain its promise and bounded outer wait; a synchronous release failure or end rejection is unknown and never retried as ownership recovery. Release alone isn't close. Fulfilled end never clears prior fault. No subsequent pool query, re-reserve or new admin to 'verify cleanup'. Source/root/server checks around this close remain, but no query can run on a released connection.

## Journal and retention rules; explicit early-refusal choice

Journal grammar stays initdb/server/test; runner maps to role test. Existing journal control-state rules at69–129 require initdb closed before server-start, identity-verified before test-graph, test closed before two quiescence samples/admin-close/server-close, plus output EOF before child-close. Emit controls only from actual corresponding observations, not invented success events to satisfy journal. Runner raw bytes stay private; sanitized control tap records actual exit/EOF/close facts only, and output validation decides outcome separately. Observe/bind/drain first; any journal/mirror fault burns unknown and prevents further effects. A fixed allowlisted route code is separate terminal diagnostic; journal failure reason remains PRIVATE_TEST_CLUSTER_REFUSED. No secret-bearing raw error text or child output escapes.

Existing attachPrivateTestChildJournalV1 can only be reused for initdb/server if actual observer ordering and raw output policy are demonstrated; it must not control ownership or be installed before full bindings. Prefer a tiny new-mode sanitized control tap for allthree, with no unvalidated runner forwarding; don't add a second partial lifecycle authority. A journal stream-end event is not stream-close evidence; full owned observer remains necessary.

IMPORTANT discovered existing grammar constraint: journal.control('server-close') before test completion/two samples/admin-ended burns journal eligibility (lines99–101), so the optional no-setup-dispatch shutdown path cannot simply reuse success controls. Recommendation for4B: conservatively RETAIN the cluster/server on every refusal before genuine runner cleanup eligibility; do not implement the optional predispatch shutdown shortcut. The adopted requirement permits shutdown only with independent no-descendant/unchanged-baseline proof; it does not require taking that shortcut. This avoids a journal policy change, fabricated test-close/quiescence or a signal after a journal fault. Record truthful fixed refusal/retention and owned handles' known current facts. Root must decide this explicit conservative choice before4Brelease; if it requires shortcut implementation, separately refine journal refusal-path semantics and tests rather than silently weakening existing successful path.

On unknown/cancel/limit/journal/admin/pin fault: no new phase, no DROP (constructor never owns generated-resource DROP), no server signal. Close/settle only own input/client/pin handles as possible without racing pending operations; continue bounded actual child drain. No destroy/unref as proof, no SIGKILL/group signal, no child PID adoption, no recursive deletion or deleting diagnostics. A refused run may leave live originals/resources for Root accounting. Unknown final Source/journal/root close means no final success, even when server had already legitimately stopped before the late fault; do not pretend to undo elapsed effects.

# Adopted delivery-window mechanism (historical addendum)

# Task4B delivery-window addendum v1 — NOT ADOPTED / NOT SOURCE RELEASE

## Exact correction

Map step7's phrase “issuer rechecks current backend pair, baseline/source/root/server ... after runner spawn” must not mean a new SQL query, full baseline capture, or source.check() inside the delivery window. Perform ALL full Source/SQL acquisition and rechecks BEFORE spawn. Between returned recipient original and its writer.end(frameBytes), use only the actual bound original witnesses, current bounded process observations, a separately acquired PRIVATE finite delivery-file metadata pin set, own root/data/tool metadata checks, private fault/clock ledgers, and exact DATA encoding. Reuse the already captured immutable backend pair and baseline rows/hashes as retained observations; do not label them a fresh postspawn SQL observation.

Binding plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-22-manual-orphan-owned-setup-v1.md:155` explicitly requires full M/T/bootstrap/dependency acquisition/recheck before recipient spawn, then only original/process/delivery-pin checks and frame construction until writer.end; reader has5s from its own initialization. Original plan's old setup field listing at137–139 is superseded by adopted4A required constructorAdminBackend; this timing clarification changes no schema.

## Why existing Source.check is not a delivery check

Current helper SHA2568bc6c5eb82b1d3487c0eb7a06a5fd380f3fc6ab873b2fb4e8e77276cb8dd845c:

* holder at997–1200 exposes exact {source,check,close}; no hidden FD or quick-check accessor is available to a caller.
* check at1003–1008 calls pins.check twice, all retained owner.check functions, identityCheck, and both current build observers.
* pins.check at711–716 iterates all retained directories/files/absences. Directory checks may readdir/compare members at630–635; file checks read and hash full contents at657–673.
* identityCheck1155–1157 calls common(), which runs Git through1047–1055; that Git helper itself brackets execution with full pins.check. Owner/build observers are not a five-second metadata-only surface.
* close at1011–1015 may itself call full check before closing. Neither check nor close is suitable as a nominally cheap “current Source” action in a successful issuance window.

Do not modify this holder, weaken its existing full checks, add a public fastCheck, reuse a borrowed holder's internal handles, or simply rename its call. The full holder remains alive and independently owned through the run, while a tiny second pin set provides only the narrower delivery predicate.

## Private delivery pins: exact scope and acquisition

In the actual private constructor function, add one nested delivery-pin closure owning NEW FDs it opens itself before spawn. It is not exported and takes no caller owner/client/permission callback. Fixed M is already physically derived/admitted by this constructor; all leaf paths below are fixed literals under that M. No wire/caller path chooses a file. This requires changes only in the future4B constructor/test File Map, no helper API/body or journal changes.

Pin exactly the11 existing Source comparison files, in their existing source.files key order:

These are supplementary delivery pins, NOT a replacement full tracked Source/dist/package/build/target inventory. Do not add a fabricated missing integration file to obtain them. Actual holder already refuses missing required files until realTask6. T's full contents/package inventories remain exclusively covered by actual full holder and receiver independent qualification; the delivery check makes no new claim that every T file was rechecked inside five seconds.

Acquisition BEFORE spawn:

* Hold all deduplicated fixed path ancestors for these leaves from / through M and the fixed scripts/tests/src subdirectories; no recursive enumeration. There are14 unique directories for the specified fixed M layout: /, /Users, /Users/setrox, /Users/setrox/ai, /Users/setrox/ai/setrox, workspace/releases, M, M/scripts, M/scripts/__tests__, M/tests, M/tests/execution-attempts, its/helpers, M/src, M/src/installation. Require exact physical paths, directory/non-symlink types and adopted owner/protection rules. Open each O_RDONLY|O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW, retain FD immediately. Use the actual canonical account/workspace strings, not HOME. No general caller-selected ancestor tree.
* Open each leaf O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK after metadata/type/size prechecks; retain FD immediately. Bracket its full positional read/hash with lstat/fstat and the held ancestor checks. Limit size to the existing comparison-file16MiB bound; require regularfile/nlink1/adopted owner/mode/device constraints.
* Compare dev/ino/uid/gid/mode-permission-bits/nlink/bytes/mtimeNs/ctimeNs/SHA256 to that actual full holder.source.files entry, not an independently invented expected digest. Save exact local full-mode and birthtime identity as local evidence where applicable, alongside the actual FD. Bytes/hash are computed and compared before spawn only.
* After this separate acquisition, run the actual full holder.check() one final time before the final SQL/prelaunch work, then recheck the supplementary FDs/named paths metadata. Any mismatch burns refusal/unknown under existing fault rules. The closure has no way to bless a failed full holder; its creation is lexically after actual holder acquisition.

During delivery, check only current lstat(namedPath)/fstat(heldFd) for these11 leaves and14 held directories. For files require same dev/ino/type/uid/gid/mode/nlink/size/mtimeNs/ctimeNs and captured local identity; for immutable selected subdirectories require captured identity/protection plus mtime/ctime (no readdir). Shared ancestors outside M use stable identity/protection rather than global mutable timestamps, so unrelated changes under /Users do not become a fabricated full Source listing check. Every component remains non-symlink. There is no readFile/readSync/full-byte hash/readdir/Git/import/resolution in this closure during delivery. Do not open duplicate Node/PG/root/data resources just for this window. The actual constructor already owns fixed NODE and admitted PG/tool-chain pins plus mutable cluster root/data FDs from4B setup; use their PRIVATE metadata-only comparisons against retained identity and fixed paths. If an existing tool check also rehashes bytes, explicitly split its private full-check and finite-metadata operations inside constructor, without changing full admission policy. Mutable cluster root/data retain dev/ino/uid/gid/mode/birthtime; do not enforce their mtime/ctime across expected creation. Do not apply that relaxation to the11 immutable Source leaves.

This pin set detects ordinary rename/replacement/metadata-observable drift in its exact scope. An open FD is not an immutable file snapshot; metadata equality is not a fresh full-byte hash, a whole-directory membership proof or protection against privileged same-UID restoration/racing. State exactly that narrower claim.

## After spawn: exact allowed sequence

1. Spawn one prescribed recipient with original fixed arguments/options. Assign the returned ChildProcess immediately; retain its actual FD4 writer without losing the original if a property access throws. Immediately bind the adopted full original/error/exit/close/all-present stdout/stderr/FD4 witness. No clock/hash/pin/SQL/journal-IO work may precede these binding attempts. Check delivery elapsed time and retained original snapshots immediately before/after EACH complete observation. Only the new child pin becomes the corresponding child field. Run one finite delivery-pin metadata pass for11files/14ancestors plus already-owned fixed executable/tool chain and mutable root/data metadata; check all relevant current original snapshots/private ledgers. No journal/file/SQL await is inserted between final checks and end. A synchronous end throw or an overrun during end is unknown even if bytes may have entered the pipe. Yield promptly to event loop to allow actual stream progress. Receiver must consume/close inherited4 before its own expensive Source acquisition and enforce its existing5s from reader initialization. This is a bounded local staging detail, not a new journal schema or independent lifecycle authority. If all controls are emitted after end naturally, no queue allocation is necessary; tests must not rely on favorable scheduling to lose early events.

## Five-second limit: explicit operational limitation

The existing process observer is bounded per subprocess, NOT by a caller-supplied aggregate deadline. Its readProbePsV1 at1316–1321 uses three synchronous ps calls with timeout2000ms each; observeManualOrphanProcessV1 at1343–1357 also hashes the independently observed executable. One observation can therefore take more than5s in a refusal path; three do not have a guaranteed worst-case total below5s. Do not claim that our outer JS timer can preempt that synchronous work, and do not hide this by starting the delivery clock afterwards.

Smallest compatible choice: retain these existing actual observers unchanged, check absolute prespawn elapsed before/after each, and NEVER CALL writer.end(frameBytes) after the5s cutoff. If any observer/check blocks past the cutoff, the run becomes sticky unknown and no valid secret frame is issued; the receiver may already have timed out. This is finite fail-closed admission of successful delivery timing, not a guarantee the parent returns control in five seconds or every legitimate host load succeeds. Do not widen/retry a slow run. Actual Task7 must demonstrate the complete sequence and real EOF/close on the admitted host without per-run threshold changes.

If Root instead requires each internal process observation to be preemptibly constrained by the remaining5s budget, the existing public API cannot supply it. That would require a separately reviewed private deadline-aware process observer/parser factoring and an explicit File Map decision; it is NOT silently implemented here, is not necessary for fail-closed cutoff semantics, and must not be introduced by duplicating the entire process/Source owner or adding a public caller clock/options argument. This is the only timing implementation choice left open.

A receiver can initialize later than spawn, and OS scheduling/stream close can still be delayed after a timely end. Therefore parent cutoff and receiver5s are independent necessary conditions; timely end is not a claim of timely receiver EOF+close. Actual writer/reader witnesses decide. No heartbeat, ACK query, extra secret channel, replay or deadline reset is proposed.

## Refusal, later full checks and narrow tests

On a delivery pin/process/clock/ledger failure, enter abort state, never emit a valid frame, and settle only the original owned writer/input as permitted (one empty end if needed to convey EOF/refusal, tracked separately from issued-frame state); do not destroy/unref as proof. Retain original child/server/current facts, bound actual drains, and do not retry the spawn/FD/frame. An empty/failed issuance does not certify no descendant setup effect until actual complete receiver refusal joins. Existing Root-adopted pre-cleanup retention applies: no server signal or DROP on unknown. Any Source.close that may perform full checks is performed only after leaving/aborting the issuance interval and under own cleanup rules, not disguised as a delivery check.

After actual FD4 delivery/close is observed, full holder checks may resume outside the window. For constructor waiting on its runner, do not reread 'initial baselines must be unchanged' concurrently with legitimate child provisioning: those catalogs can now correctly contain the generated objects. Use actual phase/cleanup policy, then require full restored baselines after runner cleanup. A full postdelivery Source failure remains unknown and cannot revoke already emitted bytes; recipient's independent full Source/cluster validation before effects is mandatory. These are non-atomic observation boundaries, not simultaneous Source/SQL custody.

Focused additional component tests should target actual private consumers via permitted diagnostic-only bindings, not unlock the lexical issuer with a successful holder:

* Pre-spawn preparation completes fullcheck/query seams before native spawn; no postspawn fullcheck/query/import/resolution call. Count reached call ordering, not a fake fullconstructor pass.
* Delivery metadata helper exact11file/14directory scope, zero read/hash/readdir during delivery, heldFD/path mismatch or metadata change rejects. Its pre-spawn hash comparisons use real crypto over bounded nonsecret fixture DATA and cannot fabricate Source-holder acceptance.
* Actual private window/observer consumer crosses5s while a contained observation is executing: no frameend afterwards; local observer completion is retained diagnostic only. Clearly label synchronous-overrun return-time limit.
* Actual writer consumer ends at most once, rejects late clock/FD/pin faults, tracks partial/end-throw uncertainty, and does not call a valid-frame retry or new child.
* Journal control staging is bounded32, preserves actual order, never forwards raw bytes and burns unknown on overflow/flush failure. This tests local staging only, not actual Source/SQL/native custody.

Final test registration counts belong to later4B author/release, not this clarification. ActualTask7 is the mandatory positive for full Source acquisition, retained pre-spawn catalogs/backend connection, measured real process observations, timely frameend and actual reader/writer/child closure. No synthetic complete Source/SQL owner can stand in for it.

Raw ps args retain the relative runner/integration operands above, because those are the actual planner/spawn operands. `process.argv[1]` resolves to the physical fixed file; it is not evidence that ps had an absolute operand. Compare both actual representations to their prescribed forms; do not manufacture command hashes by joining process.argv. Direct test PID must equal the original handle PID. No relay, nested test worker or extra child operand.

Define P as NODE-directory + `:` + real account home `/.local/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`. New-mode producers supply exactly `{PATH:P,LANG:'C',LC_ALL:'C'}`. This is already the stock normalizer's result. Permit only Darwin `__CF_USER_TEXT_ENCODING` as three canonical uppercase-hex uint32 fields separated by colon: each is `0x0` or `0x` plus one nonzero uppercase-hex digit and at most seven further uppercase-hex digits; first equals current UID in that unpadded form. Integration alone additionally requires Node-generated `NODE_TEST_WORKER_ID:'1'`, rejects NODE_TEST_CONTEXT; its producer does not set worker ID. No inherited HOME, TSX_DISABLE_CACHE, NODE_OPTIONS/NODE_PATH, GIT_, PG/selected/internal-production/DYLD/LD redirection or other keys. New no-loader children get a fresh baseline object, never the integration's effective environment. Git subprocess sanitization is fixed and separate. Preserve old13 environment and4GiB behavior.

Constructor keeps the existing lexical PG tool prefix `/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin/` and exact planner initdb/server argv. Root admits and pins the actual opt-link→Cellar tool resolution/hash/version; do not incorrectly require that Homebrew's opt ancestor is nonsymlink. The exception is this exact independently admitted link chain, never an arbitrary caller alias.

Consumer independently observes the live server using Slice1 ps-before/comm/ps-after. Require its original constructor PPID, same UID, admitted physical PostgreSQL17 executable/hash and exact raw command:

`<lexical PG prefix>postgres -D /tmp/setfarm-task6a-pg.<suffix>/data -h 127.0.0.1 -p <port> -k /tmp/setfarm-task6a-pg.<same suffix> -c unix_socket_permissions=0700 -c shared_buffers=32MB -c max_connections=50`

Suffix is ASCII alphanumeric, lexical root<=128 UTF8 bytes; canonical decimal port1024..65535 excluding5432. Parse this fixed grammar then recompose raw equality. The only temp alias is system `/tmp`→physical `/private/tmp`; pin that alias and physical ancestors. Retain lexical root for actual PostgreSQL settings comparison; derive physical root for generated installation paths and private-directory checks. PG may retain lexical data_directory/socket settings: require exactly the constructor argv spelling, not guessed physical spelling. Require realpath equivalence to held physical root/data. No other aliases.

Root/data directory FDs stay owned. Installations is physicalRoot+'/installations'; I is installations+'/'+validatedUUID. No pathname comes from wire input. Hold mutable owner-directory identity (dev/ino/uid/gid/mode/birthtime), not unchanging mtime across expected creates. Hold immutable Source/package directory listings separately. Constructor's original server handle supplies custody; receiver ps observations crosscheck it. Never adopt a later PID, replacement server, refreshed start time, root, OID or digest. This is ordinary same-UID anti-reuse evidence, not protected native custody.

## 5. Constructor and runner ownership

Constructor's new-mode code path is separate from old13 effects/environment/cleanup. Require8GiB+64MiB before root/password/cluster and again before allocations; stock factory/activation checks stay. Create existing lexical root0700, private password0600, journal, reserve port, execute exact initdb with current full original/streams witness. Only initdb normal0+close+EOF permits server spawn. Bind original server witness before fallible work; readiness30s verifies current original remains live plus actual settings/current database and user postgres, PG17, system identifier, postmaster start. Derive actual backend PID from each owned admin connection.

Capture baselines before generated effects: exactly3 DB rows (postgres/template0/template1), <=128 roles,<=256 memberships, total canonical<=65536 bytes; request one extra row to detect overflow. No pg_authid/password data. DB row order `name,oid,ownerOid,allowConnections,connectionLimit`; role `name,oid,superuser,inherit,createRole,createDb,login,replication,bypassRls,connectionLimit,validUntilUTC`; membership `roleOid,memberOid,grantorOid,adminOption,inheritOption,setOption`. validUntilUTC null or exact UTC microseconds or stock literal `infinity`/`-infinity`. Sort DB/roles by UTF8 name then numeric OID; memberships numeric tuple. Hash JSON.stringify(array) without LF. Retain full rows locally, do not guess default role OIDs.

Only a lexical constructor closure after actual initial resources/readiness/baselines/Source checks can mint RunnerSetupFrame. It captures the returned original runner and writer before observation, rechecks current facts and ends FD4 once. No exported issuer callback. Runner consumes/closes FD4, verifies own/immediate constructor/server pins, independent Sources/Cluster and baseline equality, and creates previously absent physical installations0700. Its private admin connects only to the derived fresh /postgres endpoint after all checks. Constructor keeps its own separate admin. No ambient URL or wrapper relay.

Runner calls one fixed route in the existing TS fixture. It owns provision, activation and integration originals and privately retains receipts/output; it never pipes unvalidated child stdout/stderr into constructor journal. The constructor journal keeps existing role vocabulary initdb/server/test and reason vocabulary: map new route failure to PRIVATE_TEST_CLUSTER_REFUSED in journal and emit a separate fixed sanitized route code. Journal/display faults burn unknown. New-mode final success additionally requires runner cleanup receipt, actual runner original settlement, independently restored baselines/quiescence, constructor admin close, original server shutdown/stream settlement, own source/root/journal closure and Root's original constructor settlement.

## 8. Receipts, APIs and refusal behavior

Expose only finite inert codecs/readers/observers plus owned-source acquisition in existing MJS:

`encode/decodeManualOrphanRunnerSetupFrameV1`, `encode/decodeManualOrphanOwnedPhaseFrameV1`, `holdManualOrphanOwnedSourcesV1()`; `readManualOrphanOwnedFd4V1(expectedReceiver)` with exactly runner|integration, each accepting only its specified schema/purpose; helper direct entry uses a PRIVATE one-read decoder accepting only probe or the four helper purposes, then dispatches by validated schema/purpose (no phase CLI and no attempt to guess purpose before reading); `observeManualOrphanOwnedChildV1(actualChild,purpose)` with closed initdb/server/runner/provision/activate/integration/old_entry_positive/new_entry_refusal enum. The latter returns only current snapshot, first-terminal settled promise and copied bounded output; no signal/spawn/SQL selector. Actual spawn sites and phase functions remain private. Keep Slice1 APIs/probe semantics unchanged.

TS fixture adds exactly `runManualOrphanBootstrapRouteV1():Promise<void>` and `runManualOrphanBootstrapIntegrationV1():Promise<IntegrationResult>`; all genuine state is lexical/module-private and initialized once from actual frame. No caller-provided fixture/handle can mint ownership. Existing component exports are unchanged and retain their component labels.

Phase stdout receipt (one canonical frame<=16384 bytes, stderr empty) exact keys:

`schema:'setfarm.manual-orphan-owned-phase-result.v1',run,purpose,frameSha256,pid,parentPid,fd4Closed:true,sourcePinsClosed:true,result`

Purpose result exact ordered shapes:

- provision: `{state:'schema-verified',installation:CreatedI,factoryOwnerClose:'fulfilled'}`.
- activate: `{state:'activation-verified',installation:ActivatedI,selectionBefore:'schema-committed-unactivated',selectionAfter:'stale-refused',databaseModuleClose:'fulfilled'}`.
- old_entry_positive: `{state:'old-entry-verified',maintenance:Release,target:Release,installationId:UUID,sameClient:true,entryAssert:true,databaseAssert:true,close:'fulfilled',closedGetter:'refused'}`.
- new_entry_refusal: `{state:'source-mismatch-refused',maintenance:Release,target:Release,installationId:UUID,mismatch:'maintenance-sha-vs-target-origin',close:'fulfilled',closedGetter:'refused'}`. No falsely instrumented `stage` field.

Integration normal bounded stock reporter output plus exactly one canonical marker line prefixed `MANUAL_ORPHAN_INTEGRATION_RESULT `, payload keys:

`schema:'setfarm.manual-orphan-integration-result.v1',run,frameSha256,pid,parentPid,oldEntry:<old result>,newEntry:<new result>,sourcePinsClosed:true`

Marker is written by the test after integration function returns, before normal test completion. Stock no-isolation reporter output may be checkmarks/info summary rather than TAP; treat it as bounded reporter text, not a fixed whole-stdout JSON encoding. Match exactly one complete LF-terminated line beginning with the fixed prefix at column zero; parse/re-encode its payload, reject duplicate or malformed prefix occurrences (including partial/trailing ambiguities), validate digest/PIDs/results, and require original exit/close0 plus known single-test success. Runner accepts the marker plus actual original completion, never marker alone. Assertion failure can have reporter output but is not forwarded publicly.

Runner standalone receipt keys:

`schema:'setfarm.manual-orphan-runner-result.v1',run,frameSha256,pid,parentPid,installationId,oldEntry,newEntry,cleanup,sourcePinsClosed:true`

cleanup exact `{databaseAbsent:true,ownerRoleAbsent:true,runtimeRoleAbsent:true,baselineRestored:true,adminClose:'fulfilled'}`. Receipt never proves future parent/server settlement. Every parent checks original PIDs/issued digest and fresh witness after receipt/pin closure.

Fixed helper/runner refusals: FRAME_REFUSED, OWNER_REFUSED, SETUP_REFUSED, CASE_REFUSED, CLEANUP_UNKNOWN, each prefixed `MANUAL_ORPHAN_`; empty stdout, exact code+LF stderr, exit1. Cleanup uncertainty dominates. Constructor keeps existing journal/stage framing and sanitized mapping. Never reflect malformed private child output; drain/hash bounded bytes locally and report fixed code only. Runtime credentials stay in generated0600 record and stock private selected state; no raw buffer/URL/error/frame in artifacts. No secure JS-string erasure claim.

## 9. Distinct real-child deadlines and joins

Attach original spawn/error/exit/close and every present stdout/stderr/FD4 listener immediately after spawn before fallible hashing/pinning/resume. Missing channel never prevents binding present channels. Original terminal success requires actual exit followed by agreeing close code0/signalnull, natural EOF+close both outputs, and required FD4 finish/end/close with no errors. initdb/server have NO FD4 requirement. Nonzero fully observed exit is a refusal, not automatically a missing-lifecycle unknown. Error, conflicting/duplicate/reordered events, data after EOF, invalid binding, output overflow or deadline burns sticky unknown. First settled promise is immutable history; current snapshot may later become unknown. Recheck it after every await and immediately before/after source-close/final result.

| Purpose | Deadline from spawn unless stated | Per-channel drained-byte cap |
|---|---:|---:|
| Existing probe | unchanged10s | unchanged65536 |
| initdb |60s |262144 |
| server readiness |30s, live-original check | server lifetime262144 |
| server whole lifetime |960s |262144 |
| runner |840s |262144 |
| provision |300s |262144 |
| activation |180s |262144 |
| integration |300s |1048576 |
| each mechanical child |120s |262144 |
| cleanup SQL sequence |30s from start | no raw output |
| explicit SQL end |5s requested API timeout; bounded outer wait | none |
| eligible server SIGINT join |30s from signal | existing lifetime cap |

Hold at most65536 copied diagnostic bytes/channel; incremental hash/count covers all drained bytes even overflow. Receipt markers fit16KiB. No destroy/unref as evidence of close. Deadlines are finite refusal thresholds; no per-run widening. Runner840s contains300+180+300+30 plus overhead; server960s includes readiness and shutdown. Long synchronous Source reads do not evade deadlines: elapsed-time checks at boundaries supplement timers.

On unknown/cancel/limit/journal/client/pin fault: no new phase, no DROP, no server signal; settle/close only own input/client/pin handles as possible, continue bounded drain and report retained current original facts. Do not abandon a pending awaited operation and perform cleanup concurrently. Race timeouts mark unknown; eventual resolution is collected, never green. No SIGKILL/group kill, PID adoption or signal to setup/test children. Natural settlement after deadline supplies diagnostics only. Missing close remains retained unknown. Root owns the actual external constructor graph/capture and must account for unresolved originals. Tests simulate missing forever-close instead of intentionally orphaning a real process.

## 10. Exact cleanup and retention

Only acknowledged provision+activation, actual originals/FDs/source/client closures, cases settled, stable cluster/server/root/Source/OIDs and no uncertainty are cleanup-eligible. A fully closed deterministic assertion mismatch may clean exact resources but still returns CASE_REFUSED; absent closure report or any unknown retains. Partial factory/activation failures never clean. Retain all filesystem diagnostics; no unlink/rm/recursive cleanup.

Runner uses its already captured admin against /postgres. Reverify full settings/system ID/postmaster start/original server; exact DB owner/OID; exact role OIDs/flags/no memberships; captured baselines plus only one generated DB/two roles. Known client backends are only constructor+runner admin PIDs, obtained from their actual connections. Require no generated-DB session and no unknown client backend; standard background types follow existing quiescence list. No pg_terminate_backend/FORCE/sweep/repair.

Order, with identity recheck immediately before every effect, fixed identifier quoting and5s statement/lock timeout:

1. Recheck complete ledger/catalog/session baseline; DROP DATABASE exact generated name outside transaction, without FORCE.
2. Require acknowledged return AND exact name/OID absent. Any lost ack/error stops all subsequent drops even if later read-only diagnostics find absence.
3. Recheck original role identities/memberships; DROP ROLE exact runtime; verify name/OID absent.
4. Recheck owner identity; DROP ROLE exact owner; verify name/OID absent.
5. Require full DB/role/membership baselines equal originals twice100ms apart, same cluster/root/source; await runner admin.end, close retained pins, emit runner receipt and settle original runner.

Constructor independently verifies complete restored baselines plus existing two-sample quiescence requiring only its admin and three DBs. Await its admin.end. Only then call existing bounded `settleOwnedPrivatePostgresV1` on its ORIGINAL server witness, and additionally require the new complete server stdout/stderr witness, normal exit+close and no late fault. Journal.finish/close and root/Source close are final joins; Root still observes original constructor EOF/exit/close. No receipt/PID absence substitutes for those.

If setup was never dispatched, only independently proven no-descendant/unchanged-baseline refusal can use original-server shutdown under the same admitted cleanup scope. Otherwise retain cluster/server/resources on unknown. Drops are ordinary fresh-cluster ownership operations, not atomic OID-based protection against hostile concurrent same-UID writers; exclusive ownership remains mandatory.
