## Correction: the sole explicit close and its only post-close fence

Prior map line86 is wrong where it proposes `entry.assert` refusal after close. Replace that instruction in the future implementation with this sequence; the old file stays frozen. Root's binding ruling forbids all post-close `entry.assert`, `entry.prepare`, and `db.assert` calls. The entry assertion can start an unjoined hidden `pgClose`, so observing its rejection is not an acceptable close fence.

For the old-entry positive, complete both intended prepare calls, both entry assertions, both database assertions, same-getSql-reference checks, and required Source/selection/record qualifications while the namespace is still open. Retain the exact actual db namespace imported from the authenticated physical T URL; never import a second query/fragment/module copy for cleanup. In the one lexical finally, burn the explicit-close obligation before invoking that captured namespace's `pgClose` once. Retain its actual returned promise immediately. Immediately after invocation, before awaiting that promise, call only the captured namespace's `getSql()` and require a synchronous refusal. Await that same promise's fulfillment under the owned finite deadline/current fault ledger. Then call only the same `getSql()` again and require refusal. No post-close entry assertion, entry prepare, database assertion, SQL, reconnection, retry or reselect is permitted.

Apply the same close fence to activation and new-entry refusal: their pre-close mechanics differ, but the post-close observations are solely immediate and post-await captured `getSql` refusal. An unexpected resolved getter or close throw/rejection/pending deadline burns uncertainty; it never authorizes another close or renewed assertion. The finally obligation still joins the actual retained promise when an immediate fence observation fails; a failed assertion must not bypass that join. On a synchronously thrown close, no close promise exists to await and no retry is permitted. Capture/report that uncertainty without claiming fulfillment. The explicit-close sequence does not certify inaccessible stock controller/client end-call counts. The actual Source-defined expected new-entry rejection may itself have internal close behavior; this addendum forbids adding post-close assertion calls to manufacture more of it.

Narrow tests should instrument the actual private helper consumer's captured namespace operations, proving ordered calls `pgClose -> getSql(refuses) -> await same promise -> getSql(refuses)`, one explicit close attempt even on failure, and zero entry/assert/prepare/database-assert calls after that invocation. Such finite namespace/lifecycle observations are not a successful Source holder or a full owner emulator. Real same-module stock behavior and all actual Source qualifications remain Task7 obligations.

## Proposed one inert collector facade

Add exactly one public function to the existing MJS only after Root adopts this refinement:

`observeManualOrphanOwnedIntegrationChildV1(actualChild)`

Declared arity/Function.length1; valid calls require `arguments.length === 1`. There is no purpose, options, clock, parser, callback, owner, source, path or quick-check parameter, no alias and no public scanner. It invokes the SAME private child-observer engine with the fixed integration policy and a module-private literal collector-enable flag. Only a primitive fixed collector-enable flag exists initially. Construct the guarded private scanner inside that engine AFTER all obtainable original/stream lifecycle bindings and pause attempts, BEFORE data binding/resume; no fallible scanner allocation/initialization may precede retention and binding of actual channels. Catch initialization failure, mark the existing binding uncertainty, leave scanner disabled/refused, and still attempt every remaining independently bindable data/drain slot. No initialization throw escapes early or abandons the original. Do not accept an arbitrary scanner object/function even through a newly exported helper. No launch, signal, destroy, unref, FD close, source acquisition, SQL or authority transfer occurs in the facade. An emitter used in a finite component test proves observation behavior only; the real runner must supply the actual original it just spawned and retain its separate lexical ownership.

Existing `observeManualOrphanOwnedChildV1(actualChild,purpose)` and `observeManualOrphanOriginalChildV1(child)` retain their exact parameter, witness, output, limits, error and lifecycle behavior. They do not enable this collector, gain an output key, acquire stricter reporter grammar or change invalid-purpose handling. Shared engine refactoring must preserve their existing regression suite; no duplicated observer implementation in TS.

The new frozen witness has exactly `{settled,snapshot,output}` in that order. `settled` and `snapshot()` retain the existing owned snapshot shape with purpose integration: `{purpose,state,exit,close,stdout,stderr,fd4,reason}` and existing frozen nested lifecycle facts. `settled` is historical one-time engine settlement, not a grant to ignore later faults or the collector. In particular it can resolve before content is accepted, and never changes after resolution. No new success field is added to these snapshots.

For the new facade only, `output()` returns a fresh frozen plain object with exact ordered keys `{stdout,stderr,integration}`. stdout/stderr are fresh copied Buffers of at most65536 bytes each, exactly the retained prefixes of their actual channels. Buffers are detached copies, not frozen typed arrays; mutation cannot change later output or scanner state. The third value is deeply frozen detached DATA with exact ordered keys `{state,marker,summary}`:

* state is exactly pending, observed, or refused.
* marker is null unless state is observed. When observed, it is the actual strict decoded IntegrationResult from the sole canonical marker, with the existing ordered fields `schema,run,frameSha256,pid,parentPid,oldEntry,newEntry,sourcePinsClosed` and existing nested validation/freezing. No synthetic success marker is constructed from counts or caller expectations.
* summary is null unless state is observed. When observed, its exact ordered numeric keys are `{tests,suites,pass,fail,cancelled,skipped,todo}` and values exactly `{1,0,1,0,0,0,0}` parsed from the complete observed report. No duration, raw line, error text, expected result or extra key is exposed.

Internal decoded candidate/parsed counts may exist before publication, but pending/refused output exposes both as null. This avoids presenting an early or invalidated marker as accepted DATA. Previously returned deeply frozen DATA remains historical; a later output call must reflect any current fault. Consumers must not cache observed across awaits or use historical settled/output after a later snapshot becomes unknown.

Build copied prefixes before the final current-state calculation; then use the engine's current snapshot path, including both digest-copy checks and monotonic check, before deriving integration state. A copy/allocation/hash/clock/internal scanner exception (including initializer, byte scanner, decoder infrastructure rather than deterministic invalid input, or finalizer failure) latches the engine's existing binding uncertainty, continues safely bound drain, and prevents observed. If output cannot allocate its promised copies, throw only fixed `MANUAL_ORPHAN_CLEANUP_UNKNOWN`, not raw exception text or substitute empty-success buffers. Other facade setup failures return the best retained witness as existing owned binding semantics permit; no early failure abandons independently bindable channels.

## Invalid arity, drain and error mapping

Invalid arity must not throw before binding/drain. It selects the SAME fixed integration300000ms/1048576 policy, but starts the new witness unknown with existing reason `purpose` and the collector refused. It must not read, stringify, enumerate, coerce or retain extra arguments. With no first argument, no stream exists to bind and the returned witness is unknown/refused. With an actual first child plus extra hostile arguments, attempt every original lifecycle slot and independently obtainable stream/error/data slot exactly as the current owned engine does, then safely resume each channel having error+data coverage. Do not remove listeners, destroy or close the original. Invalid-arity state cannot recover. This is an explicit new-facade policy; existing invalid-owned-purpose policy remains unchanged.

Choose a narrow separation of content refusal from lifecycle uncertainty, refining the prior map's provisional suggestion to mark every parse error as witness unknown. Deterministic scanner grammar/count/duplicate/unsupported-format refusal sets an absorbing private content-refused latch, but does NOT falsify the child lifecycle by changing its snapshot to unknown or inventing a new public reason. The new integration state is refused immediately, while the ordinary engine continues counting/hashing/draining/joining actual originals. Engine state observed merely means the original lifecycle was observed; its code may still be nonzero, as already true of the existing observer. The new integration state observed has the stronger success conditions below. This separation requires Root's adoption; it is not an unannounced implementation change.

One necessary conservative exception follows from the ACTUAL existing codec: `decodeOwnedDataV1` catches all failures and throws the same FRAME_REFUSED, so its exception cannot distinguish invalid JSON/schema from an internal allocation/runtime failure. Do NOT infer that distinction from error.message, and do NOT add a second cloned canonicalizer/parser merely to classify it. Treat ANY thrown existing decode call as engine binding uncertainty -> CLEANUP_UNKNOWN, mask marker/summary, and continue all bind/drain/join obligations. A fully returned strict decoded object may advance grammar; obvious deterministic line/prefix/count/length/UTF8 violations detected by the scanner itself can remain semantic refusal. Thus some malformed canonical payloads conservatively produce UNKNOWN rather than CASE. This is the smallest fail-closed mapping satisfying Root's internal-fault rule with the actual unchanged codec; no codec/API change is required. Runtime exceptions thrown while enforcing any deterministic predicate likewise take the UNKNOWN path, never its boolean-refusal path.

Exact consumer mapping and precedence:

1. Any current engine unknown, invalid-arity/binding/hash/clock/deadline/output-limit fault, stream/FD4/original error, duplicate/bad lifecycle, missing close or own close uncertainty -> `MANUAL_ORPHAN_CLEANUP_UNKNOWN`. It dominates content errors and any earlier positive marker/summary/settled value. Later natural completion supplies diagnostics only.
2. Fully current observed lifecycle with zero signals, matched exit/close, but nonzero code, nonempty stderr, missing/invalid marker, invalid reporter grammar/counts or content-refused latch -> `MANUAL_ORPHAN_CASE_REFUSED`, except any codec/internal exception already captured by rule1. It never supplies integration observed and, under the pending conservative retention choice, never grants generated cleanup merely because the process closed.
3. Pending lifecycle plus no fault/content refusal -> pending; wait only within existing finite deadline. A pending lifecycle plus content refusal exposes integration refused but does not manufacture lifecycle completion; continue actual drain/join. At deadline/missing close, rule1 wins. Do not emit CASE while a required owner remains unjoined.
4. Actual valid IntegrationResult whose run/frameSha256/PIDs/releases/installation differ from runner's retained issued facts -> CASE_REFUSED after known joins, otherwise rule1. The arity1 collector cannot know issued facts and does not authenticate those correlations. The runner compares them independently using actual original PID/parent and issued-frame digest before accepting cases.

Malformed stdout marker caught as a deterministic scanner predicate is content refusal at this consumer; a thrown existing decoder is conservative UNKNOWN as explained above. Neither is emitted as setup transport FRAME_REFUSED. Incoming FD4 frame errors elsewhere retain their existing FRAME_REFUSED mapping. No new public diagnostic code or interpolated reporter/JSON error text is added. Source/owner qualification outside this collector keeps its existing fixed mapping; collector observed is never Source/holder/admin authority.

## Exact bytes, line recognition and bounded storage

Every actual stdout Buffer admitted by the engine's existing nonproxy/Buffer check is counted and hashed in full, even beyond retained prefixes, output limit, content refusal or prior unknown. The scanner runs synchronously in that SAME data callback before its final settle call; no second listener is attached after the facade returns. It must neither keep the incoming Buffer/view nor depend on caller mutation of output copies. Stream strings, proxy Buffers or inaccessible byteLength remain engine channel faults, not coerced text. A late data chunk after end/close still follows existing accounting/fault rules and cannot recover integration observed.

Keep the existing independent channel caps1048576 and diagnostic-prefix cap65536. No full-stream concat/string, array of all lines/chunks, or second1MiB retained copy is permitted. Additional retained scanner storage is at most one16384-byte COMPLETE marker line (33-byte prefix plus at most16351-byte canonical payload includingLF), a256-byte ordinary/reserved-line prefix, finite UTF8/reserved-pattern matching state, bounded counters and one decoded bounded marker object. Copies made for codec validation are bounded by that same single-candidate size; do not accumulate candidates. The decoded object is bounded by the existing16384-byte codec limit AND the stricter collector payload limit16351, not an arbitrary JSON value. Keep no per-line history. Count/hash remain the existing all-byte evidence. On an internal storage failure latch engine binding uncertainty; on a deterministic too-long reserved candidate latch content refusal and discard candidate bytes while continuing drain.

Recognize lines by literal byte0x0a only. CRLF, ASCII NUL, ESC/ANSI and all other ASCII controls includingTAB/DEL are refused; ordinary text must be valid UTF8. Use finite streaming UTF8 validation across arbitrary chunk boundaries (or equivalent fixed-size bounded decoder slices); never reset validation at chunk edges, replace malformed bytes, or retain a chunk-size-proportional string. A split multibyte checkmark/info glyph is accepted when complete; invalid/overlong encoding or incomplete UTF8 at EOF refuses. No BOM, whitespace normalization, trim-based acceptance or Unicode lookalike marker is accepted. The only newline admitted in marker payload is its canonical terminalLF through the existing codec.

The reserved marker stem is ASCII `MANUAL_ORPHAN_INTEGRATION_RESULT` (32 bytes); canonical prefix is that stem plus exactly oneASCIIspace (33 bytes). A valid occurrence starts at columnzero, before the test/report block, and appears exactly once. After collecting that prefix, retain through the firstLF, then pass precisely the payload includingLF to existing `decodeManualOrphanIntegrationResultV1`; its existing decode/reencode equality proves canonical ordered keys, schema/booleans/identity syntax/deep freeze. Do not JSON.parse a prefix or separately relax JSON whitespace/key order. COMPLETE marker-line length must be at most16384 bytes INCLUDING the33-byte fixed prefix, payload and terminalLF. Therefore payload length1..16351 includingLF is necessary here even though the shared codec alone accepts up to16384. Check the stricter whole-line cap before codec invocation; do not widen the codec or silently admit a16417-byte marker line. Empty payload, missing/extra space, CR, duplicate keys, different order, invalidUTF8, suffix afterJSON beforeLF, extraLF insideJSON, oversize or malformed DATA refuses.

Use fixed finite byte-pattern state across every stdout byte to catch the reserved family `MANUAL_ORPHAN_` anywhere in a line. An occurrence is legal only as the start of the one valid canonical marker at columnzero; a second occurrence, indentation, embedded occurrence, malformed reserved-family line or reserved stem inside purported ordinary text is refused. No substring search limited to copied-prefix bytes. AtLF/EOF, a line consisting of a nonempty proper prefix of the exact reserved stem (optionally preceded by ASCIIspaces) is also refused as a truncated marker rather than ignored ordinary output. Once the reserved family has appeared, any later mismatch/EOF is refusal. A coincidental shorter prefix which diverges before reaching that family can be ordinary text, subject to reporter exclusions; this precisely avoids treating every ordinary word beginning M as a marker. An unfinished ordinary line at EOF is refused too, so no trailing-byte ambiguity is accepted.

Ordinary preamble lines may be longer than256 or65536 bytes, but must be LF terminated, valid UTF8, contain no reserved marker family, and not be a reserved reporter line below. Keep only the prefix needed to classify the line and finite pattern/UTF8 state while skipping the remainder. A marker beginning on a NEW line after more than65536 ordinary bytes must still be recognized. A marker embedded deep in one long ordinary line is refused, not accepted as columnzero. For a reserved marker line exceeding16384 bytes, refuse as soon as its bound is exceeded and keep draining without retaining the remainder.

## Grounded fixed reporter grammar

The successful complete stdout language is:

`ordinary-preamble-line* -> canonical-marker-LF -> exact-PASS-line-LF -> tests-LF -> suites-LF -> pass-LF -> fail-LF -> cancelled-LF -> skipped-LF -> todo-LF -> duration-LF -> naturalEOF`

The preamble allowance is a deliberate bounded parser policy to support the required after64KiB-marker case; the calibration did not show a large preamble. It carries no success evidence. There is no ordinary preamble line between marker and PASS, between summary lines, or after duration. No blank/trailing line after duration, interleaved second marker/test, or post-summary diagnostics are accepted. A future observed legitimate reporter variation requires explicit Root review; no automatic TAP fallback or permissive silent broadening.

Exact future PASS line (UTF8 glyph U+2714, oneASCIIspace after glyph, exact literal name, oneASCIIspace before parenthesis):

`✔ manual orphan bootstrap v1 preserves old entry and refuses maintenance source (<D>ms)`

There is exactly ONE such line, and it MUST follow the canonical marker. `<D>` is ASCII nonnegative decimal matching `(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,11})(\.[0-9]{1,12})?`, with the decimal point literal; no sign/exponent/NaN/infinity/leading zero/extra spaces. Maximum token25 bytes. Durations need not match each other, are not success counters and are not public summary fields; admission/deadline remains the actual observer monotonic clock. This finite decimal spelling contains the observed2.674667 and58.297125 and proposes a bounded future policy, not proof of all possible Node reporter spellings.

The next seven lines are EXACTLY, with UTF8 U+2139 and oneASCIIspace after glyph and label, canonical integers and this order:

`ℹ tests 1`

`ℹ suites 0`

`ℹ pass 1`

`ℹ fail 0`

`ℹ cancelled 0`

`ℹ skipped 0`

`ℹ todo 0`

Then exactly `ℹ duration_ms <D>` andLF, using the same decimal grammar. Parse each count into a number only after matching its literal bounded token; all are safe integers with the exact required values, not truthy strings or rounded large numbers. Every required summary line appears exactly once. Duplicate/out-of-order/omitted/unknown info labels, alternative capitalization, count2, leading-zero1, noninteger/negative counts, suites1, skipped1, fail1, malformed duration, another PASS, an alternative name or any failed-test line refuses even when a canonical marker is present.

Reserve reporter-shaped lines in the preamble rather than treating an unsupported result as ordinary text. Classification starts at columnzero or after leadingASCIIspaces solely for detecting refusal, never for accepting grammar. Reserved starts include any checkmark/cross/test-status glyph `✔`, `✖`, `✓`, `✗`, `ℹ`, `▶`, `►`, `-`, or the ASCII tokens `TAP version`, `ok`/`not ok` followed by space/end, `Bail out!`, `# Subtest:`, `# tests`, `# suites`, `# pass`, `# fail`, `# cancelled`, `# skipped`, `# todo`, `# duration_ms`, and an ASCII digit run followed by `..` (TAP plan). These are refused in preamble and must match the exact expected line in the report block. The digit-run detector is finite state, not a retained unbounded number. Reserving the whole indicated status glyph/line family is intentionally conservative. All lines beginning the info glyph are reserved, so a malformed/unknown summary is never skipped as noise. Full TAP, TAP mixed with the calibrated block, alternate test lines, and supported-looking counts without the exact PASS all refuse. A neutral ordinary line cannot contribute PASS/count credit.

At the first data after the completed durationLF, refuse regardless of whether it is ordinary text, anotherLF or a reserved token. At stdout natural end, run guarded finalization BEFORE setting the channel end flag and settling the engine, finalize UTF8/line/candidate and require complete grammar; missing finalLF or any incomplete reserved prefix/candidate/report refuses. A close without end remains engine unknown. Stderr must have zero total bytes; inspecting only its copied prefix is insufficient. Stderr bytes cause content refusal immediately but still undergo full engine counting/hash/drain.

## Publication and current late-fault rule

The private scanner tracks grammar progress and a sticky content-refused bit. It does not independently claim an original close, time completion, FD4 finish, successful child execution or client closure. Output state observed is permitted ONLY when all of these are current together: engine snapshot observed, matching exit and close both exactly code0/signalnull, stdout and stderr both natural end+close, FD4 finish+peerend+close, timely engine completion with current monotonic/hash facts valid, stderr total0, all-byte caps honored, sole canonical marker and complete exact report grammar with no content refusal. Otherwise it is pending when still possible, or refused on any known content/lifecycle fault. Valid report+marker while streams or FD4 are open is pending/null/null.

Content refusal is sticky. Engine unknown is sticky. A post-settlement stream/child/FD4 error, duplicate end/close, bytes after end, failed digest copy, backward/unavailable clock or other existing engine fault makes the next integration output refused/null/null even if a previous settled snapshot and previous output were observed. Timer stopping after timely completion does not waive late faults; retain the existing checkClock semantics that still detect clock failures/backward movement. Do not rearm a deadline after genuine completion or alter existing policy. No consumer may infer present acceptance from the resolved historical promise alone.

The caller should join the original, then obtain new output and current snapshot, compare marker with its own issued frame/original/installation facts, and recheck current snapshot/output at any subsequent effect boundary. There is no atomic guarantee against a later event after a synchronous observation; those checks are current observations, not custody or an irrevocable success grant. A later fault denies future phases/DROP and preserves diagnostics. The actual runner's full source/catalog/client/resource ledger remains necessary; this inert scanner grants none of it.

## Finite meaningful test proposal, not executed

Implement tests against the complete actual private scanner through the proposed facade and existing engine, using finite Buffer streams/EventEmitters and the current canonical codec. Do not replace Source admission, create a fake successful holder, implement a successful constructor/runner owner emulator, launch genuine setup or remove guards. Tests can naturally access narrowly extracted actual private mechanical-close consumer bindings under the already proposed contained harness; no new public test callbacks. Retain all existing owned/legacy regression controls unchanged and assert their output keys remain exactly stdout/stderr.

Propose sixteen focused collector groups, each bounded and with explicit assertion targets:

1. Calibrated positive language with future literal test name, codec-produced canonical marker, exact counts and full engine lifecycle: pending until last required join, then observed and actual decoded marker/equal summary. This proves DATA/lifecycle parsing, not genuine integration acceptance.
2. Marker after more than65536 bytes of neutral LF-terminated preamble and one long neutral preamble line; diagnostic stdout prefix lacks marker but scanner observes it and complete all-byte hash/count matches supplied bytes. Memory instrumentation/assertion targets retained bounded state, not an invented1MiB-success buffer.
3. Enumerate each byte split of one finite canonical successful transcript, including splits inside marker prefix/JSON/UTF8 glyphs/newlines/durations; identical accepted data. Also feed one-byte chunks and one whole Buffer.
4. Marker-only, exact-report-only, report before marker, and canonical marker with wrong literal test name: never observed. This is the decisive marker-is-not-success control.
5. Duplicate marker before/after PASS, malformed family line, embedded/indented marker, partial stem atLF/EOF, prefix split at bound, extra marker suffix and complete-line16385-byte overflow: deterministic refused; all remaining bytes still counted/drained.
6. Canonical-codec negatives within otherwise valid report: duplicate/reordered keys, wrong schema/types, invalidUTF8, extra whitespace, missingLF, payload above16351 or whole marker line above16384; no acceptance from permissive JSON parse. Assert scanner-detected size/prefix/UTF8 refusals separately from thrown existing-codec rejection, which conservatively burns snapshot unknown because its catch-all hides provenance.
7. PASS duplicates, failure/status glyph, unexpected test name, extra test/suite, missing/count-changed summary and every pairwise summary-order swap: refused; no marker-derived count credit.
8. Unsupported complete TAP and TAP followed by otherwise valid calibrated block, reserved summary in preamble, unknown info label and neutral text interleaved after marker: refused.
9. Decimal grammar boundary table, CRLF/ANSI/NUL/BOM/invalid and truncatedUTF8, trailing bytes/extraLF after duration, and unfinished ordinary final line: refused without unbounded retention.
10. Exactly1048576 stdout total versus one byte beyond using bounded preamble; below/at limit can be content-valid, overflow must yield engine unknown and refused. Confirm stderr byte1 refuses content and still counts/hash/drains; channel overflow escalates unknown.
11. Stderr not ended, stdout missing close, FD4 missing finish/peerend/close, child exit without close: pending until the current engine's finite unknown condition; no report completion can supply absent lifecycle facts.
12. Invalid facade arity0/2 with a hostile extra Proxy whose traps must not run; when a first emitter is supplied, every independently bindable readable drains under fixed integration policy and output is refused. No foreign close/destroy/unref.
13. Synchronous data on resume is captured because scanner was installed first; synchronous data during binding inherits existing hash/binding uncertainty rather than acquiring fabricated empty digest. Cover independently bindable channels after one binding slot fails.
14. Late original/stream/FD4 error, duplicate close, data after EOF and digest/clock failure after historical observed: historical data stays frozen while current snapshot/output refuses. No late event restores observed.
15. Output copies detached; exact ordered new output/integration/summary keys, frozen nested marker/summary, old facades unchanged; prefix-copy/internal scanner exception latches binding uncertainty and emits only fixed cleanup-unknown on an output throw.
16. Actual caller comparator rejects otherwise canonical run/frameSha/PID/parent/release/installation mismatch using bounded DATA, and content CASE is never emitted before actual joins; current unknown dominates every content refusal, including indistinguishable internal/invalid-DATA codec exceptions. No fake actual Source or SQL is required to reach these predicates.

Add four narrow close-consumer groups: normal explicit-close order with immediate/post-await getter refusal; pending/rejected/thrown close never retried or upgraded; failing immediate getter still joins retained close promise; sentinels on entry.assert/prepare/db.assert proving zero calls after explicit close. These groups replace the erroneous post-close assertion proposal rather than adding a new production API. Root can merge overlapping existing cases; final exact registrations belong to Root's tests release, not this design artifact.

The genuine one-test TS file, actual fixed-T setup/activation, selected module import ordering, same captured module close, all8 controls, source/native/process/backend custody and generated cleanup are still mandatory actual Task7/frontier work. A parsed positive component transcript is not that run. Existing diagnostic60218 is reporter calibration only; do not rerun it for this proposal or call it real fixture acceptance.

## Serialized Task5A / Task5B / Task5C dependency order

Root's proposed split is coherent and recommended, subject to separate exact Root plan/tests release for each unit and no Task5 Source writer while Task4B is writing. Each milestone keeps the existing global guards in place; no fake successful fixture, absent export call, dead placeholder, public owner hook or premature real integration registration fills a dependency gap.

1. Task5A owns only the existing MJS helper and existing route tests. Implement/review the complete exact fixed integration facade, guarded private collector inside the existing engine, error/output distinction and finite collector/lifecycle regression tests first. It is an inert API even without the real integration test file, Source holder success or actual TS route. The existing canonical IntegrationResult codec, engine and fixed integration policy already exist; no Task5B/5C export is needed. Preserve existing owned/legacy contracts and all old tests. Complete5A means this actual callable API and its finite tests/review are complete, not genuine integration acceptance.
2. Task5B owns only that same MJS helper and route tests after5A is delivered/reviewed under Root's serialization. Implement the complete four private actual handlers and actual strict helper context/dispatch, including approved exact physical-negative expectation changes. It consumes existing Source/frame/observer/stock-T APIs, not future TSfixture exports. The mechanical close sequence in this addendum is binding design for these consumers. No helper handler calls or imports a nonexistent TS route, integration registration or Task4C entrypoint. Guarded direct dispatch remains fail-closed; current writer-worktree physical negatives stop at the actual admission predicate. Root must explicitly adopt the two changed expectation codes, not merely suppress those controls. If that adoption is withheld,5B completion is blocked at that exact test/behavior contract;5A remains independent.
3. Task5C owns only the existing TSfixture and route tests, after both actual MJS prerequisites are complete. Implement the exact two complete zeroarg exports, genuine phase ownership/delivery, original joins, existing collector consumption, issued marker correlation, ledger/cleanup and close protocol. Import the already implemented5A facade by its single adopted name. Use the complete5B handlers through actual spawned helper entry; do not replace them with callbacks/emulators. Keep the existing component exports and baseline import inertia. No helper API redesign should be deferred to5C: if actual Source inspection exposes another necessary helper contract, stop that dependent seam for an explicit bounded Root refinement instead of an unresolved import or placeholder. Real integration registration/wrapper/runner wiring remain later4C/Task6 work.

There is no identified Source dependency blocking this order: codec/engine precede5A; stockT APIs and genuine helper entry precede5B; both helper and collector precede5C. Remaining design adoptions can block their specific unit: exact facade/grammar/error policy before5A; context/handler test scope and two negative expectations before5B; delivery-pin/cleanup ownership/test scope choices before5C. A tests harness may reference only exports already real in its unit; it must not create a fake production export for future units. WholeTask5 is NOT complete until5A+5B+5C are complete, reviewed and satisfy their scoped checks; then Root can release4C/Task6. Component completion does not advance mandatory actualTask7 acceptance. ## Root adoption points and unchanged pending choices

Actualfullreview findings VERBATIM follow; sameauthor owns exactsmallestfix aftercausalRED, notyetSourcepermission:

- **I1 — Stop re-expiring a child after its timely complete original joins.** `scripts/run-private-postgres-tests.mjs:426`–`:430` always applies the outer prespawn purpose deadline, even after the current shared witness is fully observed. `currentOriginals()` at `:863`–`:869` revisits every retained original throughout later phases. Thus an initdb that completes normally at, for example, 2 seconds becomes sticky UNKNOWN as soon as later Source/SQL/runner work reaches 60 seconds from its spawn; a runner can similarly expire during later cleanup after timely receipt/joins. The shared observer already latches timely completion and stops only its completed deadline (`tests/execution-attempts/helpers/manual-control-orphan-bootstrap-child-v1.mjs:1410`, `:1435`–`:1438`), so the outer check contradicts that lifecycle rather than revealing a new child fault. A legitimate later phase can be refused and its live server retained solely because a finished earlier phase's clock kept burning. **Smallest fix:** latch the first complete current purpose-specific witness only while the outer prespawn deadline is still valid; after that latch, continue monotonic availability/backwards-time and current late-fault checks without reapplying the completed purpose expiry. Preserve spawn/binding time charging, refusal for completion first observed too late, independent readiness/delivery limits and sticky prior faults. **Focused regression:** extend the actual private spawn/check consumer fixture: complete initdb within its outer 60-second budget, advance the contained owner clock beyond 60 seconds during a later phase, and require healthy current checks; then inject a late stream/original error or backwards/unavailable clock and require UNKNOWN. Also test spawn/binding consumption that makes first observed completion late. The existing completion test at `scripts/__tests__/private-postgres-test-cluster.test.js:1452` tests the shared witness consumer, not this outer completed-clock behavior. No full-owner emulator or native orphan is required.

- **I2 — Join the retained operation even if timer setup fails.** In `scripts/run-private-postgres-tests.mjs:531`–`:535`, `start()` has already returned and its promise is assigned to `pending` before `setTimeout`. If timer creation throws, execution skips `await pending`, catches/marks uncertainty, and clears `pending` in finally although the original operation is unresolved. The initial-acquisition catch at `:657` then sees no pending work and invokes close; the established admin's close path at `:620` likewise permits release/end. A pending reserve may resolve and assign `reserved` after pool close has begun, or a query may still use the connection while it is released/ended. Its eventual rejection also lacks the intended await/join. The analogous final-close path at `:628`–`:633` requests `pool.end` before timer creation; a timer throw skips awaiting that already-started end promise, allowing the owner to leave admin close while its actual end remains unresolved. This violates the explicit retained/eventually-collected operation rule; sticky UNKNOWN alone does not repair resource ownership. **Smallest fix:** separate fallible timer setup from unconditional collection of each already-started operation. Mark timer faults but still await/join the exact retained reserve/query/end promise; keep pending true until that actual operation settles, guard timer cleanup through the same uncertainty ledger, and preserve release-once followed immediately by end-once with no intervening query/Source check. Do not replace this with timeout abandonment or Promise.race cleanup. **Focused regressions:** inject a throw from the contained timer seam after an unresolved reserve and after an unresolved query have started; assert pending remains true, release/end stay zero, and eventual resolve/reject is collected without promotion or unhandled rejection. Separately inject the 5-second close-timer setup throw after end has started; close must remain unsettled until that exact end promise settles, with one release/end and sticky UNKNOWN. Existing tests at `scripts/__tests__/private-postgres-test-cluster.test.js:1573` and `:1602` cover timers firing and operation faults, not timer-creation failure. These are narrow admin-operation tests, not actual SQL or full-owner success.

## Required implementation semantics

I1: latch a complete current purpose-specific original witness only while the OUTER prespawn clock still satisfies its original purpose budget, so spawn/binding time remains charged and first completion seen too late still refuses. Only after that valid latch stop reapplying that completed purpose expiry. Continue checking monotonic clock availability/backwards movement and CURRENT late original/stream/writer/binding/cap/hash faults with sticky uncertainty. Do not let shared-observer success alone bypass the outer budget, revive an earlier failure, or change readiness30s, issuance5s, runner840s, initdb60s, server960s or server-shutdown30s. Use complete current witness state rather than partial events.

I2: after an actual reserve/query/end is started, retain and unconditionally await THAT exact promise regardless of fallible timer construction. A timer setup failure marks sticky UNKNOWN but never clears pending or permits concurrent release/end before the actual reserve/query settles; collect its later fulfillment/rejection without unhandled rejections or promotion. Clear pending only after actual settlement. Guard timer cleanup exceptions with the same sanitized UNKNOWN ledger. For final close, release at most once immediately followed by pool.end at most once with no intervening query/Source/await; even if 5s timer setup fails, retain and join actual end before initial/repeated close can settle. Preserve query fencing, actual backend identity, no reconnect/refresh and clean-closure ownership semantics. A timer fault is uncertainty, not permission to abandon started work.

## SQL ledger and private qualification seam

Keep SQL ownership a small natural common consumer needed by all four phases, not a general injected adapter. Production composition dynamically imports the actual package after admission, performs a current checkpoint after that import, calls its genuine constructor with exact privately derived URL/fixed options, then immediately gives the returned pool to the private ledger before shape checks. This is local acquisition retention, not a borrowed constructor/runner client. The ledger never obtains a Source holder or mints phase authority.

Proposed private ledger operations are capturePool(actualReturnedPool), reserve(), query(kind,expected), close(), and current(); fixed kind literals only identity/databases/roles/memberships plus internal bounded read-only transaction/control steps. No arbitrary SQL string, query callback, connector, external clock or owner is an argument. Its state retains pool, reservation, actual pending promise, immutable pair, SQL-stopped/release-attempt/end-attempt flags and absorbing unknown. Production calls current full checkpoint immediately around each awaited ledger operation; it owns all full Source/process/record checking. Unit tests call this same ledger directly and prove ONLY query/lifecycle behavior. They never pretend those checkpoints occurred.

Own immutable pair is captured on actual reserved connection after validating identity. Subsequent identity/catalog SELECTs must contain/recheck that same current own-row metadata. A fixed one-row envelope is suitable: own pg_backend_pid/backend_start/datid/usesysid plus bounded catalog rows; the zero-membership result must STILL carry own-pair metadata, not skip identity because rows=[]! Use UTC microsecond text and host(inet_server_addr()); no other-user fields or protected settings in runtime SQL. Finite fixed SELECT templates live privately. Keep transaction-local setting/BEGIN/COMMIT mechanics distinct from qualified catalog DATA; they do not manufacture an identity row. Never turn a pooled reconnection into a fresh accepted pair.

The validator builds fresh plain canonical rows from actual postgres result values. Do not hand the driver's Result subclass or driver metadata directly to the strict baseline canonicalizer, and do not relax that canonicalizer's prototype/key checks. SQL OIDs should be losslessly normalized from canonical decimal text to the required numeric range; systemID stays canonical uint64 text. Validate every response bound before projection; one extra row detects overflow. Query returns/thenables must be actually retained, awaited and mapped conservatively, not classified by message as successful close.

A query is busy until its actual retained promise settles. Deadline/onclose/checkpoint fault burns unknown and forbids further query/phase/public prepare; no Promise.race timeout may release/end concurrently while query is pending. Collect eventual settlement without clearing unknown. close() stops new SQL permanently, joins pending work if any before resource actions, burns each attempt before invocation, releases once synchronously and immediately calls end({timeout:5}) once with no intervening query/await. Release throw still burns unknown; only owned safe cleanup may continue, never new SQL. Unknown end/onclose/deadline cannot be upgraded by later fulfillment. End's internal timeout may terminate connections; strict actual elapsed/current ledger checks must not call that a normal proven close. Missing forever-settlement is simulated in components, never created as a real process here.

Initial provision qualification uses its authorized admin/initial baseline; activation admin uses generated stage. Mechanical qualifier uses only stock local runtime credentials and the generated DB grant. Capture own pair is REQUIRED in all these actual qualification connections; no foreign-admin census is added. For mechanical phases, close qualification fully, call actual T read-only activated verifier and require its normal return after its internal finally, recheck full Source/process/root/records, THEN select installation/import ordinary namespace/public prepare. No qualification or stock-verifier client reopens after public prepare or captured close.

## Complete four-handler control sequence

Provision: require no selected env, genuine own context/initial cluster/baselines/capacity; import actual fixed-T factory/records/schema/migrations within admitted closure. Actual createFreshInstallationV1 receives exactly derived adminUrl/baseDirectory/actual Tsha once. Retain returned owner immediately, initialize once, compare actual returned origin and owner.origin/full33 identities, verifyRuntime once with its REAL void result. Read stock committed PreparedState/physical directory/generated catalogs, project exact CreatedI, close actual owner once and every own qualification/client/pin/Source before result eligibility. Rejection before owner return can follow partial effects: unknown/retain, no fake owner, retry, repair or DROP. No selected ordinary db import in provision.

Activate: validate CreatedI and immutable seven records/schema stage/current generated catalog; stock activation absence must be real. Own admin qualification closes before stock effect call where no retained qualification is needed. Set internal selected env once BEFORE actual T selection/db import; capture same physical namespace; selected activation null/unprepared getSql predicates precede actual activateFreshInstallationManifestAV1({directory,adminUrl}). Use unchanged private stock transaction registration; no supplied transaction. Actual activated verifier returns only after its own close. Require old absence snapshot now stale/refused, seven old pins unchanged/two new pins valid and distinct schema/activation catalog hashes. No reselect/reset or later use of obsolete absence as success. Capture database namespace as soon as acquired and close once in finally using only getter fences.

Old positive: complete runtime qualification/actual T activated verifier and their closures FIRST. Select I, import exact T db/entry, initial getSql refusal; prepare, capture public SQL reference, entry.assert and db.assert/reference equality; prepare again and assertions/reference equality again. Full current Source/process/record checkpoints remain required before close. One captured explicit pgClose; immediate getter refusal; await retained promise even if immediate fence failed; post-fulfilled getter refusal. Never post-close entry/assert/prepare/db.assert. No claim this proves whole public service/workflow startup.

New refusal: same real T activated prerequisites and private qualification closures, current authenticated M!=origin T, select before M ordinary imports, capture exact M db namespace. Initial getSql refusal, actual M entry.prepare rejection under valid prerequisites, preserve qualified mismatch without treating arbitrary loader/record failure as the expected negative. Stock Source ordering supports the mismatch interpretation; do not claim an inaccessible branch trace from error text. Actual captured close/getter fence as above. Integration parent later correlates old/new results in5C; this helper does not invent parent success or carry an admin secret.

Actual dispatch only matches the four literal purposes already admitted by the union decoder. Integration and RunnerSetup stay FRAME-refused by that decoder; legacy probe remains on its unchanged actual old path. Require owned transport for nonprobe AFTER canonical close; legacy nonprobe refuses OWNER before new account/Source work. Physical M parent/name/current-account gate rejects actual W before Source holder/process/native/SQL. This ordering preserves all existing no-effect controls below. Success payload is encoded with actual existing phase result codec only after known actual owner/client/pin closes and current fault check; caller stdout is never populated with private state/raw SQL/record errors.
